I found the CoRL 2024 Instruction for Reviews page. Here's what the search results show:

## Review Criteria

Submissions will be evaluated based on the significance and novelty of the results, either theoretical or empirical, and results will be judged on the degree to which they have been objectively established and/or their potential for scientific and technological impact, as well as their relevance to robotic learning.

## Reviewer Qualifications

Reviewers need to have expertise in robotics, robot learning, machine learning and complementary fields, as demonstrated by a record of previous publications in the area, and ideally have a minimum of 3 first author publications in major robotics conferences and journals (e.g. CoRL, RSS, ICRA, IROS, IJRR, TRO, RAL) and core machine learning venues.

## Process Details

_source line 415 · 2026-05-20T11:38:49.213093+00:00_

# CoRL 2024 Reviewer Criteria - Review Form Analysis

Based on the provided page content, I cannot extract a complete reviewer form with scoring dimensions, rating scales, or specific questions reviewers must answer.

## What is Available:

The document specifies **Reviewer Qualifications**:
- Must demonstrate expertise in robotics, robot learning, machine learning, and related fields
- Ideally possess a minimum of 3 first-author publications in major venues including "CoRL, RSS, ICRA, IROS, IJRR, TRO, RAL" and machine learning conferences
- Area chairs should nominate faculty, research scientists, researchers, and senior graduate students

## What is Missing:

The page does not contain:
- A scoring rubric or rating scale
- Numerical evaluation dimensions
- Specific questions reviewers must complete
- Point scales (e.g., 1-5, 1-10)
- Category weightings

The document focuses on review criteria (significance, novelty, objectivity, impact, relevance to robot learning), desk rejection guidelines, and reviewer qualifications—but stops short of providing the actual review form structure reviewers would use to submit their assessments.

To obtain the complete reviewer form, you would likely need to access the review submission portal directly or contact the CoRL 2024 program chairs.

_source line 443 · 2026-05-20T11:39:17.935222+00:00_

# CoRL 2023 Reviewer Form Criteria

Based on the provided content, the document outlines **evaluation dimensions** rather than a complete numerical scoring rubric. Here's what was specified:

## Core Evaluation Dimensions

Reviewers must assess six primary areas:

1. **Originality** — novelty of problems, methods, or combinations; differentiation from prior work
2. **Quality** — technical soundness, claim support, methodological appropriateness, completeness
3. **Clarity** — writing quality, organization, informativeness, reproducibility
4. **Significance** — importance of results, potential adoption, state-of-art advancement
5. **Relevance** — alignment with robot learning focus, use of learning-based methods, simulation/robot evaluation
6. **Limitations** — appreciation for honestly reported constraints; evaluation of unreported shortcomings

## Review Structure Components

The form includes three main sections:
- **Summary of Paper** — re-expressing contributions in reviewer's own words
- **Main Review** — detailed evaluation across the six dimensions above
- **Scoring** — the specific scales and numerical ranges are **not detailed** in this document

## Notable Gaps

The provided guidelines **do not specify** the actual rating scales (e.g., 1-5 or 1-10), numerical recommendations, or the exact scoring questions. The document emphasizes methodology and philosophy rather than providing the complete form template with all numerical scales.

```json
{
  "text": "\n# Reviewer 1 审稿意见：方法严谨性 + 实验有效性\n\n**论文**: \"Vision Encodes, Language Routes: Token-Level Functional Specialization in a Vision-Language-Action Model\"\n**Venue**: CoRL 2026\n\n---\n\n## 总体评分\n\n**Overall**: 3.0 / 5（Borderline）\n**Confidence**: 4 / 5（高——本人熟悉 VLA、probing methodology、representational similarity analysis）\n\n**一句话总结**: 方法论上严谨的多工具表征分析工作，但纯观察性证据与因果性措辞之间存在系统性落差，且缺乏闭环行为验证——对 CoRL 而言是关键缺陷。\n\n---\n\n## 官方维度打分（基于 CoRL 2023 Reviewer Guidelines 六维度）\n\n| 维度 | 分数 | 理由摘要 |\n|------|------|----------|\n| Originality | 4/5 | 首次对 VLA 进行 token-level 多方法（RSA + probe + attention + CKA）系统性分析，且 2×2 factorial + 3条件对比设计超越同期 VLA interpretability 工作 |\n| Quality | 3/5 | 统计工具选择合理（bootstrap CI、Cohen's d、FDR），但因果推断链不完整、部分统计假设未充分验证（详见 Weakness） |\n| Clarity | 4/5 | 论文结构清晰，31页含大量 appendix 补充实验，图表质量高；少数术语（readout token）定义略滞后 |\n| Significance | 3/5 | 发现 VLA fine-tuning 的 entry-exit instruction routing pattern 有启发性，但无 actionable connection 到模型设计改进或任务表现 |\n| Relevance | 2/5 | 纯表征分析，无闭环机器人实验（甚至无 LIBERO 模拟 rollout）。CoRL 强调 \"relevance to robotic learning\"，本文更适合 ICML/NeurIPS interpretability track |\n| Limitations | 3/5 | 论文有 Limitations section 且诚实列出了 open-loop 分析和架构数量限制，但对某些关键统计假设（Cohen's d 独立性、N_eff）的讨论不足 |\n\n---\n\n## Must-Fix 问题（影响接收决定）\n\n**N1** [critical] **缺乏闭环行为验证——CoRL 核心期待**\n- 具体位置：Section 4 全文、Section 5 Discussion\n- 问题：论文完全是 open-loop 表征分析。所有发现（instruction routing 前移、readout token 专化、middle-layer peak）均通过 probe R²、RSA、attention 统计度量，从未连接到实际任务成功率。Section 4.5 的 L1 ablation 实验度量的是 R² 变化（Table 5），不是 LIBERO task success rate 变化。\n- 为什么影响接收：CoRL call for papers 明确鼓励 \"real-robot experiments 或 simulation-to-real transferability 证据\"。论文甚至没有在 LIBERO simulation 中做 rollout 来验证所发现的 instruction routing 是否与 task completion 相关。这使得所有发现停留在描述性层面，无法回答\"这些表征变化对机器人实际工作有什么意义？\"\n- 建议修改方向：(a) 在 LIBERO 中做 ablation → rollout 实验：ablate L1 instruction-dominant heads 后测量 10 个 task 的 success rate；(b) 或验证 probe R² 与 task success rate 的 rank correlation（跨 10 个 task）。任一即可将表征发现 ground 到行为后果。\n\n**N2** [critical] **从观察性证据到因果/机制性声称的推断过度**\n- 具体位置：Title（\"Language Routes\"）、Section 4.5、Section 5 第2段（\"instruction routing\"）、Abstract（\"front-loads instruction routing\"）\n- 问题：论文核心叙事是 \"VLA fine-tuning creates instruction routing\"，但支撑证据是相关性的：\n  - Attention patterns（Section 4.4）显示训练后 L1 有更多 instruction-dominant heads，但 attention weight ≠ information flow\n  - Ablation（Section 4.5, Table 5）显示去除 24/32 L1 heads 后 readout R² 下降 0.034 (p < 0.001)，但这是 mean disruption——任何 24 头 ablation 都会造成类似破坏\n  - Table 23（matched-size ablation controls）是最接近因果证据的实验，但 instruction-dominant heads (d=0.650) 与 random 24 heads (d=0.601±0.021) 差异很小——95% CI [0.568, 0.622] 与 reference 0.650 有重叠\n  - R² 增加的 paradox（Table 5, attention heads 0-11 ablation 使 R² 升高）被解释为 \"information-computation dissociation\"（Section 4.5），但这个解释是 post-hoc 的，未经独立测试\n- 为什么影响接收：论文标题和核心 claim 使用机制性语言（\"routes\"），但证据强度仅支持 \"correlates with\" / \"is associated with\"。这种 overclaiming 在严格审稿中通常是降分因素。\n- 建议修改方向：(a) 将 \"routing\" 语言降级为 \"routing-like patterns\" / \"instruction-correlated attention redistribution\"；(b) 做 activation patching（而非 head ablation）来更精确地测试因果路径；(c) 对 Table 23 做正式统计检验（instruction-dominant vs random 的 permutation test）而非仅报告 point estimates。\n\n**N3** [critical] **跨架构泛化声称证据不足**\n- 具体位置：Section 4.6（TracVLA validation）、Table 24、Abstract（\"consistent across two VLA architectures\"）\n- 问题：\n  - 仅 2 个架构（OpenVLA/LLaMA-2-7B, TracVLA/Phi-3-mini），且均在 LIBERO 上测试\n  - TracVLA 仅用 5 个 LIBERO-Goal tasks（vs OpenVLA 的 10 个 LIBERO-Spatial tasks），任务集不同\n  - Pre-VLA baseline 不同（Llama-2 base vs Phi-3 pretrained），使得 Δ_train 不可直接对比\n  - Table 24 显示差异：peak layer L9 vs L11，profile shape \"flat plateau\" vs \"ramp→plateau\"，late-layer std 0.040 vs 0.011\n  - 从 2 个架构 × 1 个 benchmark 推广到 \"VLA fine-tuning\" 一般性现象，归纳基础薄弱\n- 为什么影响接收：这是论文 contribution #2（跨架构一致性），如果这个 claim 不成立，论文的贡献缩减为对单一模型的案例研究。\n- 建议修改方向：(a) 增加至少一个不同 backbone 或不同 benchmark 的验证；(b) 或将 claim 弱化为 \"初步跨架构证据\"（\"preliminary cross-architecture evidence\"），明确列出 Table 24 的差异作为 caveats。\n\n---\n\n## Should-Fix 问题（影响评分但不一票否决）\n\n**W1** [warning] **Cohen's d 的独立性假设在 token-level 分析中可能不成立**\n- 具体位置：Section 4.2（\"monotonic d = 0.48 → 0.61\"）、Section 4.3、Appendix Tables\n- 问题：Cohen's d 假设两组样本内部独立。但同一 sequence 内的 token representations 通过 self-attention 高度相关。论文对 RSA 使用了 task-level blocking（Section 3.4，适当），但未说明 Cohen's d 计算是否有类似的层级依赖处理。如果 token-level observations 被当作独立样本，N_eff 被系统性高估。\n- 建议：报告 demo-level 或 task-level aggregated 的 Cohen's d（先在 demo 内 averaging token representations），或使用 cluster-robust standard errors。\n\n**W2** [warning] **Task-level blocking 仅 N=10 的 bootstrap 可靠性**\n- 具体位置：Section 3.4（\"task-level blocking\"）、所有 95% CI 报告\n- 问题：以 task 为 blocking unit，仅 10 个独立 resampling units。即使用 10,000 bootstrap resamples，底层信息量仅来自 10 个 task 的排列。对于 95% CI，有效自由度 ≈ 9，bootstrap 分布可能不稳定。\n- 建议：(a) 报告 bootstrap CI 的 width 与 N_tasks 的 sensitivity（如 leave-1-task-out 的 jackknife variance）；(b) 明确讨论 10-task blocking 的局限性。\n\n**W3** [warning] **N_eff 报告不透明**\n- 具体位置：Table 1 注脚（\"N = 63,225 observations after excluding terminal frames\"）、各统计检验\n- 问题：63,225 是 token-level 观测数（10 tasks × 50 demos × ~variable tokens per frame），但对于 Cohen's d、bootstrap CI、FDR correction 等，有效独立样本量因层级依赖（task → demo → frame → token）可能远小于此。论文未提供 N_eff 的明确计算或讨论。\n- 建议：增加一个 N_eff decomposition 表格，列出每级层级的观测数和估计的 design effect。\n\n**W4** [warning] **FDR 多重比较校正细节不足**\n- 具体位置：Section 4.3（\"FDR correction at 29-30/31 layers\"）、Appendix A.8\n- 问题：未说明使用哪种 FDR 方法（Benjamini-Hochberg? Benjamini-Yekutieli?），也未说明 correction family 的范围（每个 metric 内跨 32 层？跨所有 metrics？）。不同的 family 定义会显著影响校正后的显著性。\n- 建议：明确报告 FDR method、correction family scope、以及代表性比较的 raw vs corrected p-values。\n\n**W5** [warning] **MLP probe 可能 capacity 过大**\n- 具体位置：Section 3.5（\"two-layer networks d → 256 → 7\"）、Figure 10（Appendix A.11）\n- 问题：对于 4096 维 input，2 层 MLP (4096→256→7) 约有 1.05M 参数。训练数据为 ~45 demos × ~tokens/demo per task。虽然 Figure 10 显示 linear probe 复现了 inverted-U pattern（支持结论），但以下信息缺失：(a) probe 的 train/val loss 曲线；(b) 是否有 early stopping；(c) 不同 hidden size 的 sensitivity analysis。\n- 建议：(a) 在 appendix 补充 probe 的 train/val loss gap；(b) 汇报 hidden size = {64, 128, 256, 512} 的 R² sensitivity。论文已有 linear probe 对照，这是好的，但 MLP 的 capacity 诊断仍不完整。\n\n**W6** [warning] **Instruction perturbation 的混淆效应**\n- 具体位置：Section 4.3（4 perturbation types: empty, shuffle, paraphrase, mismatch/random）\n- 问题：\"Shuffle\" 同时改变语义和 token 分布（bag-of-words 保留但顺序丢失），无法隔离纯语义效应 vs 纯表层效应。\"Paraphrase\" 可能仍保留关键名词，使得 bag-of-words overlap 混淆语义不变性。论文报告 shuffle → Δ = 0.51 vs paraphrase → Δ = 0.33，但未讨论这些差异的来源。\n- 建议：增加对 perturbation type 间 token-overlap 的定量分析（如 Jaccard similarity），以区分表层 vs 语义效应。\n\n---\n\n## 小问题（不影响接收）\n\n- **Readout token 定义滞后**：这个关键概念在 Section 3.1 才给出完整定义（\"the last action token serving as the generation point\"），但 Abstract 和 Introduction 已多次使用。建议在首次出现时加脚注定义。\n- **Figure 2 信息密度高**：6 个 panel 的 legend 字号较小，建议将 legend 独立为 sub-panel 或增大字号。\n- **引用格式**：6 条 bib entries 在 OpenAlex 中未找到（elhage2021, hewitt2019, bills2023language, templeton2024scaling, nostalgebraist2020, black2024_pi0），均为博客/非正式出版物，这是正常的，但建议确认引用的 URL 可访问。5 条有 first-author mismatch（minor，可能是 API 比对问题）。\n- **\"Instruction-dominant head\" 阈值选择**：使用 >50% attention to instruction tokens 作为阈值（Section 4.4, Appendix A.18），Table 18 显示结果对阈值稳定，但阈值本身的 justification 较弱——为什么是 50% 而非 mean + 1SD？\n\n---\n\n## 主要优势\n\n1. **多方法三角验证**：RSA、probing、attention analysis、CKA 四种独立工具指向一致结论，比仅用单一 probing 或 attention 的工作更可信。\n2. **精心设计的 factorial 对照**：2×2 (trained/untrained × instruction present/absent) + Llama-2 base 作为第三条件 + DINOv2/CLIP external baselines，是目前 VLA interpretability 文献中最系统的实验设计。\n3. **统计严谨性高于同行**：Bootstrap CI (10,000 resamples)、FDR correction、Cohen's d effect sizes 的使用远超大部分 mechanistic interpretability 论文的统计标准。\n4. **Appendix 的深度和透明度**：19 页 appendix 包含 robustness checks（seed sensitivity、subsample sensitivity、cross-benchmark probe、matched-size ablation controls），体现了对可复现性的认真态度。\n5. **Clean 发现有启发性**：Image tokens 在所有 32 层完全 instruction-blind 而 readout token 承担全部多模态整合——这是一个干净的、可后续检验的发现。\n\n---\n\n## 缺失的关键实验\n\n1. **Ablation → Rollout**：在 LIBERO simulation 中 ablate 关键 heads 后测量 task success rate（而非仅 R²）\n2. **Probe R² vs Task Performance Correlation**：跨 10 个 task 验证 per-task probe R² 是否 predict per-task success rate\n3. **更多架构**：至少需要一个非 LIBERO benchmark（如 RT-1 tasks）或第三个 VLA backbone\n4. **Activation Patching**：替代 head ablation 以获得更精确的因果证据\n5. **Temporal Dynamics**：论文分析的是训练完成后的快照，但 instruction routing 在训练过程中如何 emerge？（训练中间 checkpoint 的分析）\n\n---\n\n## 关于 CoRL 适配性的特别说明\n\n本文是一篇高质量的 VLA 表征分析工作，如果投到 ICML/NeurIPS 的 interpretability track，其方法论贡献会更被认可。对 CoRL 而言，主要担忧是：\n1. 完全没有闭环实验（CoRL 强调 \"relevance to robotic learning\"）\n2. 发现难以 directly actionable（论文未展示如何利用这些发现改进 VLA 设计或训练）\n3. 仅在 LIBERO 上验证（单一 simulation benchmark，无 real-robot evidence）\n\n如果作者能补充 N1 中建议的 ablation-rollout 实验，评分可提升至 3.5-4.0。\n\n---\n\n## Questions for Authors\n\nQ1. 是否有 ablation → task success rate 的实验数据？如果有，请在 rebuttal 中展示。\nQ2. Table 23 中 instruction-dominant vs random 的 Cohen's d 差异（0.650 vs 0.601）是否有正式统计检验？p-value 是多少？\nQ3. Cohen's d 是在 token level 还是 demo-averaged level 计算的？如果是 token level，是否考虑了 within-demo correlation？\nQ4. 是否有训练中间 checkpoint 的分析，以观察 instruction routing 的 emergence dynamics？\nQ5. TracVLA 使用 5 个 LIBERO-Goal tasks 而 OpenVLA 使用 10 个 LIBERO-Spatial tasks——这种不对称是否影响了 Table 24 的可比性？\n",
  "reviewer_id": "1",
  "project": "vla-ssd"
}
```

routing\"）、Abstract（\"front-loads instruction routing\"）\n- 问题：论文核心叙事是 \"VLA fine-tuning creates instruction routing\"，但支撑证据是相关性的：\n  - Attention patterns（Section 4.4）显示训练后 L1 有更多 instruction-dominant heads，但 attention weight ≠ information flow\n  - Ablation（Section 4.5, Table 5）显示去除 24/32 L1 heads 后 readout R² 下降 0.034 (p < 0.001)，但这是 mean disruption——任何 24 头 ablation 都会造成类似破坏\n  - Table 23（matched-size ablation controls）是最接近因果证据的实验，但 instruction-dominant heads (d=0.650) 与 random 24 heads (d=0.601±0.021) 差异很小——95% CI [0.568, 0.622] 与 reference 0.650 有重叠\n  - R² 增加的 paradox（Table 5, attention heads 0-11 ablation 使 R² 升高）被解释为 \"information-computation dissociation\"（Section 4.5），但这个解释是 post-hoc 的，未经独立测试\n- 为什么影响接收：论文标题和核心 claim 使用机制性语言（\"routes\"），但证据强度仅支持 \"correlates with\" / \"is associated with\"。这种 overclaiming 在严格审稿中通常是降分因素。\n- 建议修改方向：(a) 将 \"routing\" 语言降级为 \"routing-like patterns\" / \"instruction-correlated attention redistribution\"；(b) 做 activation patching（而非 head ablation）来更精确地测试因果路径；(c) 对 Table 23 做正式统计检验（instruction-dominant vs random 的 permutation test）而非仅报告 point estimates。\n\n**N3** [critical] **跨架构泛化声称证据不足**\n- 具体位置：Section 4.6（TracVLA validation）、Table 24、Abstract（\"consistent across two VLA architectures\"）\n- 问题：\n  - 仅 2 个架构（OpenVLA/LLaMA-2-7B, TracVLA/Phi-3-mini），且均在 LIBERO 上测试\n  - TracVLA 仅用 5 个 LIBERO-Goal tasks（vs OpenVLA 的 10 个 LIBERO-Spatial tasks），任务集不同\n  - Pre-VLA baseline 不同（Llama-2 base vs Phi-3 pretrained），使得 Δ_train 不可直接对比\n  - Table 24 显示差异：peak layer L9 vs L11，profile shape \"flat plateau\" vs \"ramp→plateau\"，late-layer std 0.040 vs 0.011\n  - 从 2 个架构 × 1 个 benchmark 推广到 \"VLA fine-tuning\" 一般性现象，归纳基础薄弱\n- 为什么影响接收：这是论文 contribution #2（跨架构一致性），如果这个 claim 不成立，论文的贡献缩减为对单一模型的案例研究。\n- 建议修改方向：(a) 增加至少一个不同 backbone 或不同 benchmark 的验证；(b) 或将 claim 弱化为 \"初步跨架构证据\"（\"preliminary cross-architecture evidence\"），明确列出 Table 24 的差异作为 caveats。\n\n---\n\n## Should-Fix 问题（影响评分但不一票否决）\n\n**W1** [warning] **Cohen's d 的独立性假设在 token-level 分析中可能不成立**\n- 具体位置：Section 4.2（\"monotonic d = 0.48 → 0.61\"）、Section 4.3、Appendix Tables\n- 问题：Cohen's d 假设两组样本内部独立。但同一 sequence 内的 token representations 通过 self-attention 高度相关。论文对 RSA 使用了 task-level blocking（Section 3.4，适当），但未说明 Cohen's d 计算是否有类似的层级依赖处理。如果 token-level observations 被当作独立样本，N_eff 被系统性高估。\n- 建议：报告 demo-level 或 task-level aggregated 的 Cohen's d（先在 demo 内 averaging token representations），或使用 cluster-robust standard errors。\n\n**W2** [warning] **Task-level blocking 仅 N=10 的 bootstrap 可靠性**\n- 具体位置：Section 3.4（\"task-level blocking\"）、所有 95% CI 报告\n- 问题：以 task 为 blocking unit，仅 10 个独立 resampling units。即使用 10,000 bootstrap resamples，底层信息量仅来自 10 个 task 的排列。对于 95% CI，有效自由度 ≈ 9，bootstrap 分布可能不稳定。\n- 建议：(a) 报告 bootstrap CI 的 width 与 N_tasks 的 sensitivity（如 leave-1-task-out 的 jackknife variance）；(b) 明确讨论 10-task blocking 的局限性。\n\n**W3** [warning] **N_eff 报告不透明**\n- 具体位置：Table 1 注脚（\"N = 63,225 observations after excluding terminal frames\"）、各统计检验\n- 问题：63,225 是 token-level 观测数（10 tasks × 50 demos × ~variable tokens per frame），但对于 Cohen's d、bootstrap CI、FDR correction 等，有效独立样本量因层级依赖（task → demo → frame → token）可能远小于此。论文未提供 N_eff 的明确计算或讨论。\n- 建议：增加一个 N_eff decomposition 表格，列出每级层级的观测数和估计的 design effect。\n\n**W4** [warning] **FDR 多重比较校正细节不足**\n- 具体位置：Section 4.3（\"FDR correction at 29-30/31 layers\"）、Appendix A.8\n- 问题：未说明使用哪种 FDR 方法（Benjamini-Hochberg? Benjamini-Yekutieli?），也未说明 correction family 的范围（每个 metric 内跨 32 层？跨所有 metrics？）。不同的 family 定义会显著影响校正后的显著性。\n- 建议：明确报告 FDR method、correction family scope、以及代表性比较的 raw vs corrected p-values。\n\n**W5** [warning] **MLP probe 可能 capacity 过大**\n- 具体位置：Section 3.5（\"two-layer networks d → 256 → 7\"）、Figure 10（Appendix A.11）\n- 问题：对于 4096 维 input，2 层 MLP (4096→256→7) 约有 1.05M 参数。训练数据为 ~45 demos × ~tokens/demo per task。虽然 Figure 10 显示 linear probe 复现了 inverted-U pattern（支持结论），但以下信息缺失：(a) probe 的 train/val loss 曲线；(b) 是否有 early stopping；(c) 不同 hidden size 的 sensitivity analysis。\n- 建议：(a) 在 appendix 补充 probe 的 train/val loss gap；(b) 汇报 hidden size = {64, 128, 256, 512} 的 R² sensitivity。论文已有 linear probe 对照，这是好的，但 MLP 的 capacity 诊断仍不完整。\n\n**W6** [warning] **Instruction perturbation 的混淆效应**\n- 具体位置：Section 4.3（4 perturbation types: empty, shuffle, paraphrase, mismatch/random）\n- 问题：\"Shuffle\" 同时改变语义和 token 分布（bag-of-words 保留但顺序丢失），无法隔离纯语义效应 vs 纯表层效应。\"Paraphrase\" 可能仍保留关键名词，使得 bag-of-words overlap 混淆语义不变性。论文报告 shuffle → Δ = 0.51 vs paraphrase → Δ = 0.33，但未讨论这些差异的来源。\n- 建议：增加对 perturbation type 间 token-overlap 的定量分析（如 Jaccard similarity），以区分表层 vs 语义效应。\n\n---\n\n## 小问题（不影响接收）\n\n- **Readout token 定义滞后**：这个关键概念在 Section 3.1 才给出完整定义（\"the last action token serving as the generation point\"），但 Abstract 和 Introduction 已多次使用。建议在首次出现时加脚注定义。\n- **Figure 2 信息密度高**：6 个 panel 的 legend 字号较小，建议将 legend 独立为 sub-panel 或增大字号。\n- **引用格式**：6 条 bib entries 在 OpenAlex 中未找到（elhage2021, hewitt2019, bills2023language, templeton2024scaling, nostalgebraist2020, black2024_pi0），均为博客/非正式出版物，这是正常的，但建议确认引用的 URL 可访问。5 条有 first-author mismatch（minor，可能是 API 比对问题）。\n- **\"Instruction-dominant head\" 阈值选择**：使用 >50% attention to instruction tokens 作为阈值（Section 4.4, Appendix A.18），Table 18 显示结果对阈值稳定，但阈值本身的 justification 较弱——为什么是 50% 而非 mean + 1SD？\n\n---\n\n## 主要优势\n\n1. **多方法三角验证**：RSA、probing、attention analysis、CKA 四种独立工具指向一致结论，比仅用单一 probing 或 attention 的工作更可信。\n2. **精心设计的 factorial 对照**：2×2 (trained/untrained × instruction present/absent) + Llama-2 base 作为第三条件 + DINOv2/CLIP external baselines，是目前 VLA interpretability 文献中最系统的实验设计。\n3. **统计严谨性高于同行**：Bootstrap CI (10,000 resamples)、FDR correction、Cohen's d effect sizes 的使用远超大部分 mechanistic interpretability 论文的统计标准。\n4. **Appendix 的深度和透明度**：19 页 appendix 包含 robustness checks（seed sensitivity、subsample sensitivity、cross-benchmark probe、matched-size ablation controls），体现了对可复现性的认真态度。\n5. **Clean 发现有启发性**：Image tokens 在所有 32 层完全 instruction-blind 而 readout token 承担全部多模态整合——这是一个干净的、可后续检验的发现。\n\n---\n\n## 缺失的关键实验\n\n1. **Ablation → Rollout**：在 LIBERO simulation 中 ablate 关键 heads 后测量 task success rate（而非仅 R²）\n2. **Probe R² vs Task Performance Correlation**：跨 10 个 task 验证 per-task probe R² 是否 predict per-task success rate\n3. **更多架构**：至少需要一个非 LIBERO benchmark（如 RT-1 tasks）或第三个 VLA backbone\n4. **Activation Patching**：替代 head ablation 以获得更精确的因果证据\n5. **Temporal Dynamics**：论文分析的是训练完成后的快照，但 instruction routing 在训练过程中如何 emerge？（训练中间 checkpoint 的分析）\n\n---\n\n## 关于 CoRL 适配性的特别说明\n\n本文是一篇高质量的 VLA 表征分析工作，如果投到 ICML/NeurIPS 的 interpretability track，其方法论贡献会更被认可。对 CoRL 而言，主要担忧是：\n1. 完全没有闭环实验（CoRL 强调 \"relevance to robotic learning\"）\n2. 发现难以 directly actionable（论文未展示如何利用这些发现改进 VLA 设计或训练）\n3. 仅在 LIBERO 上验证（单一 simulation benchmark，无 real-robot evidence）\n\n如果作者能补充 N1 中建议的 ablation-rollout 实验，评分可提升至 3.5-4.0。\n\n---\n\n## Questions for Authors\n\nQ1. 是否有 ablation → task success rate 的实验数据？如果有，请在 rebuttal 中展示。\nQ2. Table 23 中 instruction-dominant vs random 的 Cohen's d 差异（0.650 vs 0.601）是否有正式统计检验？p-value 是多少？\nQ3. Cohen's d 是在 token level 还是 demo-averaged level 计算的？如果是 token level，是否考虑了 within-demo correlation？\nQ4. 是否有训练中间 checkpoint 的分析，以观察 instruction routing 的 emergence dynamics？\nQ5. TracVLA 使用 5 个 LIBERO-Goal tasks 而 OpenVLA 使用 10 个 LIBERO-Spatial tasks——这种不对称是否影响了 Table 24 的可比性？\n",
```json
    "reviewer_id": "1",
    "project": "vla-ssd"
  }
}
```

- **[AGREE]** 同意并说明理由
- **[REBUT]** 反驳并提供证据（cite 论文中具体 Section/Table/Figure）

# Reviewer 2 的审稿意见（新颖性 + Related Work + CoRL Relevance）

**Overall**: 4/10 (Borderline Reject)

### Must-Fix:

**R2-N1** [critical] Finding 3（image token instruction-blindness）是 causal attention mask 的架构必然结果，不应作为核心发现呈现。
- OpenVLA 使用 causal decoder，image tokens 在序列中位于 instruction tokens 之前，causal mask 保证 image tokens 无法 attend to instruction tokens。Cross-instruction probe accuracy = chance (1/10) 是架构约束的直接推论，不是实证发现。
- 论文在 Section 4.2 标注了 "(expected)" 但 title 和 abstract 仍将此作为核心贡献呈现（"Vision Encodes, Language Routes"、"strict functional specialization"）。
- 缺失引用：(a) "Seeing is Understanding: Unlocking Causal Attention into Modality-Mutual Attention for MLLMs" (arXiv:2503.02597); (b) "Rethinking Causal Mask Attention for Vision-Language Inference" (arXiv:2505.18605)。

**R2-N2** [critical] CoRL Relevance 严重不足。无真实机器人实验、无 sim-to-real、无 closed-loop evaluation、无基于分析结果的 VLA 改进实验。Section 5 的 actionable implications 均为推测性建议，未验证。

**R2-N3** [critical] 跨架构泛化基于仅 2 个高度同质的架构（OpenVLA/Llama-2 和 TraceVLA/Phi-3），两者共享几乎相同的架构模式（dual vision encoder → MLP projector → causal LLM decoder）。缺失 flow-matching (π₀)、diffusion-based (Octo)、cross-attention 架构。

### Should-Fix:

**R2-W1** [warning] Title "Vision Encodes, Language Routes" 存在误导——实际是 positional routing（readout token 的位置决定），不是 "language routing"。

**R2-W2** [warning] Finding 1 的因果证据不够强。缺失 activation patching / causal tracing 实验。

**R2-W3** [warning] 与 Grant et al. (2026)、Häon et al. (2025) 的区分不够清晰。Grant 的 "visual dominance" 与本文 "vision encodes" 是否同一观察的不同表述？

**R2-W4** [warning] LIBERO 单一 benchmark，instruction diversity 有限（10 tasks 的差异主要在 object identity）。Cross-instruction probe 本质上是 10-way object classification。

**R2-W5** [warning] "strict functional specialization" 措辞过于绝对。

### Missing References:
1. "What Do Visual Tokens Really Encode?" (arXiv:2603.00510, 2026)
2. "Towards Interpreting Visual Information Processing in VLMs" (ICLR 2025, arXiv:2410.07149)
3. "Seeing is Understanding" (arXiv:2503.02597, 2025)
4. "Phase Diagram of Vision LLMs Inference" (arXiv:2411.00646, 2024)
5. "Understanding Language Prior of LVLMs by Contrasting Chain-of-Embedding" (arXiv:2509.23050, 2025)
6. AVA-VLA (arXiv:2511.18960, 2025)

---

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	We investigate how action-relevant structure is distributed across a VLA by combining four complementary analyses: cross-instruction probes, attention pattern analysis, RSA, and functional probes.
5	
6	%------------------------------------------------------------------
7	\subsection{Model and Data}
8	\label{sec:model_data}
9	
10	We study OpenVLA-7B~\cite{kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024}, chaining a frozen DinoSigLIP vision backbone~\cite{zhai2023_siglip,oquab2024_dinov2} (DINOv2 ViT-L/14 + SigLIP-SO400M), an MLP projector (2{,}176-D $\to$ 4{,}096-D), and Llama-2 7B~\cite{touvron2023_llama2} (6.74B parameters) that autoregressively decodes 7-D actions.
11	We use the base checkpoint pretrained on Open X-Embodiment~\cite{padalkar2023_oxe} (970K episodes spanning multiple robot platforms and task types) and evaluate on LIBERO-Goal~\cite{liu2023_libero} (10 tasks, 50 demonstrations each), which was not part of the pretraining mixture.
12	We compare three LLM conditions: (1)~VLA-trained (released checkpoint), (2)~Llama-2 base (pretrained backbone, no VLA fine-tuning), and (3)~untrained (6.74B parameters reinitialized via \texttt{from\_config()}); a fourth condition (task-specific fine-tuning on LIBERO) is compared in \S\ref{sec:learned_routing}.
13	All three share the same frozen vision encoder and projector.
14	
15	%------------------------------------------------------------------
16	\subsection{Representation Extraction}
17	\label{sec:repr_extraction}
18	
19	We extract representations from six sources spanning the VLA pipeline:
20	(a)~\textbf{DinoSigLIP+Projector}, the projector output (4{,}096-D), immediately before LLM processing;
21	(b)~\textbf{LLM image\_mean}, the mean image-token activation at each of 32 layers;

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Synthesis.}
5	VLA fine-tuning reorganizes instruction routing rather than visual geometry: training effects concentrate at the readout ($R^2$ $+0.069$, attention $+46$pp) rather than images ($\Delta \leq 1.7\%$).
6	This explains Grant et al.'s~\cite{grant2026} visual dominance finding: DINOv2 has the highest geometric alignment ($\rho = 0.692$), yet VLA readout surpasses it in decodability ($R^2 = 0.678$ vs.\ $0.634$).
7	Seven analyses support distributed L1 routing: \emph{interventional} (dose-response, $d = 0.48 \to 0.64$; counterfactual, $d = 0.63$--$0.97$), \emph{supporting} (attention gradient $0 \to 9 \to 24$ heads; $R^2$ ablation $+0.059$; trajectory $d = 0.65$), and \emph{constraining} (bootstrap CIs, $p = 0.0018$; specificity control: trajectory-$d$ ratio $1.08\times$; $R^2$ $z = -3.11$, $0$\textsuperscript{th} percentile).
8	
9	\paragraph{Mechanistic interpretation.}
10	Matched-size ablation ($d$: instruction $0.65$, random $0.60$, image $0.58$; Table~\ref{tab:specificity_control}) and dose-response (steep through 12 heads, plateau beyond 20) indicate distributed routing.
11	Counterfactual ordering follows token novelty ($\rho = 1.0$; \S\ref{sec:learned_routing}).
12	That ablation raises $R^2$ yet alters trajectories ($d = 0.65$) without increasing ground-truth error ($\Delta\text{MSE} \approx 0$; Appendix~\ref{sec:action_trajectory}) reflects an information-computation dissociation~\cite{hewitt2019control}: routing controls direction rather than precision. Random ablation yields larger $R^2$ gains ($+0.071$ vs.\ $+0.030$; $z = -3.11$, $0$\textsuperscript{th} percentile), consistent with distinct $R^2$ signatures.
13	
14	\paragraph{Functional stages and predictions.}
15	Per-layer profiles suggest three stages: routing (L0--L1; probe $99.6\%$, 24/32 instruction-dominant heads), computation (L8--L13; $R^2$ peak $0.655$--$0.678$), and decay (L14--L31, ${\sim}56\%$ of parameters; declining $R^2$ a

_source line 475 · 2026-05-20T11:49:02.411076+00:00_

1	Vision-language-action models (VLAs) fine-tune large language models (LLMs) on robot demonstrations, but what fine-tuning changes at the representational level remains unclear.
2	We decompose OpenVLA-7B across all 32 LLM layers using cross-instruction probes, attention analysis, Representational Similarity Analysis (RSA), and functional probes, with factorial trained/pretrained/untrained controls on LIBERO.
3	Three findings emerge.
4	First, VLA fine-tuning front-loads instruction routing: language pretraining concentrates instruction attention at mid-depth, while VLA training shifts this to entry-layer routing (L0--L1) with exit-layer re-engagement (L31), consistent across two VLA architectures.
5	Seven complementary analyses, including dose-response ablation (8 conditions, monotonic $d = 0.48 \to 0.64$) and counterfactual instruction analysis (4 perturbation types, $d = 0.63$--$0.97$), support instruction-conditioned action generation through distributed L1 routing, with routing dependence $2.5\times$ stronger for instruction-discriminative tasks ($p = 0.033$).
6	Per-layer profiles suggest three functional stages: routing (L0--L1), computation (L8--L13), and decay (L14--L31).
7	Second, vision encoder representations show a metric-dependent middle-layer peak consistent across two VLA architectures (RSA/probes corroborate; CKA diverges), with training effects concentrated at the readout token ($R^2$ $+0.069$, attention $+46$pp) rather than visual geometry.
8	Token-level analysis reveals strict functional specialization: image tokens remain instruction-blind across all 32 layers while the readout token serves as the primary multimodal integration site.
9

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	What does fine-tuning change inside a Vision-Language-Action model (VLA)?
5	VLAs fine-tune large language models on robot demonstrations to produce actions from visual observations and language instructions~\cite{brohan2023_rt2,kim2024_openvla}, but the decoder-only causal mask prevents image tokens from attending to instructions.
6	Instruction information must reach action-generating positions through learned attention routing, yet how fine-tuning reshapes this routing remains uncharacterized.
7	
8	Recent behavioral studies reveal that VLAs rely disproportionately on visual input~\cite{grant2026}, with language instructions largely ignored by the action pathway~\cite{haon2025}.
9	These findings suggest internal specialization but do not explain \emph{how} instruction information reaches the action-generating readout token, \emph{what} routing strategy fine-tuning learns compared to language pretraining alone, or \emph{whether} training creates new representational structure or reconfigures inherited geometry.
10	Existing analyses have not combined systematic per-layer probing with factorial trained/pretrained/untrained controls, leaving the representational mechanisms of VLA fine-tuning opaque.
11	
12	We address this gap by decomposing OpenVLA-7B~\cite{kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024}, a VLA chaining a frozen DinoSigLIP vision backbone~\cite{zhai2023_siglip,oquab2024_dinov2}, an MLP projector, and Llama-2 7B~\cite{touvron2023_llama2}.
13	We extract representations from each of 32 LLM layers at both image-token and readout-token positions, and from external baselines (DINOv2~\cite{oquab2024_dinov2}, CLIP~\cite{radford2021_clip}).
14	We compare three LLM conditions (VLA-trained, pretrained Llama-2 base, and randomly initialized) crossed with instruction presence, and apply four complementary analyses: cross-instruction probes for token-level specialization, L1 attention pattern analysis for routing mechanisms, R
…[truncated 2399 chars]

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	We evaluate OpenVLA-7B~\cite{kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024} on LIBERO-Goal~\cite{liu2023_libero} (10 tasks, 50 demonstrations each).
8	Representations are extracted from six sources spanning the VLA pipeline (\S\ref{sec:repr_extraction}): DinoSigLIP+Projector, LLM image\_mean and readout tokens at each of 32 layers, DINOv2~\cite{oquab2024_dinov2} (\texttt{cls\_token}/\texttt{patch\_mean}), CLIP~\cite{radford2021_clip} (\texttt{patch\_mean}), and branch-split (DINOv2 1{,}024-D / SigLIP 1{,}152-D before fusion).
9	Three LLM conditions are compared: VLA-trained, pretrained Llama-2 base (no VLA fine-tuning), and untrained (6.74B parameters reinitialized to random weights), crossed with instruction presence (\S\ref{sec:model_data}).
10	RSA computes Spearman $\rho$ between cosine-distance representation RDMs and Euclidean-distance action RDMs, with 10{,}000 bootstrap CIs (\S\ref{sec:rsa}).
11	MLP probes (input $\to$ 256 $\to$ 7; Adam, lr${}=10^{-3}$, 50 epochs, early stopping) report mean $R^2$ (\S\ref{sec:probes}).
12	This yields $N = 63{,}228$ observations (after excluding terminal frames without next-action labels; $63{,}728$ total); RSA uses task-level blocking ($N_\text{eff} = 10$) and probes use demo-level train/test splits.
13	All patterns reproduce with a linear probe (Appendix~\ref{sec:linear_probe}).
14	
15	%------------------------------------------------------------------
16	\subsection{Framework Validation: Token-Level Specialization}
17	\label{sec:token_specialization}
18	
19	As a sanity check on the experimental framework, we train cross-instruction probes (\S\ref{sec:cross_instruction_probes}): given the same visual frame with each of 10 task instructions, we classify which instruction was present from per-layer activations.

TracVLA 共享 \"dual vision encoder → MLP projector → causal LLM decoder\" 的架构模式，这一点我未明确指出。这使得跨架构验证本质上是在同一架构家族内的变体测试，而非真正的跨架构泛化。\n\n补充证据——论文自身在 Discussion 中承认了这一局限，且给出了明确的可证伪预测（discussion.tex line 22）：\n> \"cross-attention VLAs (π₀, Octo) **should lack L1 routing heads**\"\n\n这说明作者清楚自己的发现可能不适用于 cross-attention 和 diffusion-based 架构。但仅给出预测而不验证，对 CoRL 投稿来说不够。\n\n---\n\n### R2-W1 [warning] Title \"Vision Encodes, Language Routes\" 存在误导\n\n**[AGREE]，but with nuance。**\n\nR2 指出实际是 \"positional routing\"（readout token 位置决定）而非 \"language routing\"。这在技术上是对的：是 readout token 的位置使其能 attend to instruction tokens，而非 language tokens 主动 \"route\" 信息。但我认为论文的核心发现——VLA training 将 instruction attention 从 mid-depth 重组到 entry-exit layers——确实涉及 learned routing 的变化，不完全是 positional 的。\"Language Routes\" 的问题更在于暗示 language tokens 是 agent，而实际 agent 是 readout token。\n\n建议改为更准确的 framing，如 \"Vision Encodes, Routing Concentrates\" 或 \"Vision Encodes, the Readout Routes\"。\n\n---\n\n### R2-W2 [warning] Finding 1 因果证据不够强\n\n**[AGREE]。与我的 N2 一致。**\n\nHead ablation 是粗粒度干预。论文最强的因果证据是 Table 23 的 matched-size ablation，但 instruction-dominant (d=0.650) vs random (d=0.601±0.021) 的差异较小。需要 activation patching 或 causal tracing 才能建立更精确的因果链。\n\n---\n\n### R2-W3 [warning] 与 Grant et al. (2026)、Häon et al. (2025) 区分不够清晰\n\n**[REBUT]，论文提供了具体的定量区分，但 Related Work 确实可以更显式。**\n\nDiscussion 第 1 段（discussion.tex line 5-6）明确区分了两者：\n\n> \"This explains Grant et al.'s visual dominance finding: DINOv2 has the highest geometric alignment (ρ = 0.692), yet VLA readout surpasses it in decodability (R² = 0.678 vs. 0.634).\"\n\n这不是同一观察的不同表述：Grant 发现视觉特征在 VLA 中占主导，本文进一步解释了**为什么**——因为 DINOv2 的几何对齐确实最高（RSA），但 VLA 通过 readout token 的 routing 增加了 decodability（probe R²），而非改变视觉几何结构。这是 complementary findings，不是 redundant findings。\n\n与 Häon et al. 的区分在 discussion.tex line 28：本文研究 \"where\"（routing 的空间位置），Häon 研究 \"what\"（SAE features 的语义内容）。\n\n**但 R2 的批评有道理**：这些区分主要在 Discussion 中，Related Work (Section 2) 确实不够清晰。建议在 Section 2 增加更显式的对比段落。\n\n---\n\n### R2-W4 [warning] LIBERO instruction diversity 有限\n\n**[AGREE] on diversity limitation, [REBUT] on \"本质是 10-way object classification\"。**\n\nLIBERO-Goal 的 10 个 task 不仅在 object identity 上不同，也在动作类型和空间目标上不同（如 \"pick up X and put it in Y\" vs \"open the bottom drawer\" vs \"turn on the stove\"）。Cross-instruction probe 不仅仅是分辨 objects。\n\n但 R2 的核心观察成立：10 个 task 的 instruction 多样性确实有限。论文自身承认这一点（experiments.tex line 146: \"The N = 10 instruction set limits statistical power for detecting small effects\"）。更多样化的 instruction set（如自然语言 paraphrases、compositional instructions）将显著加强实验。\n\n---\n\n### R2-W5 [warning] \"strict functional specialization\" 措辞过于绝对\n\n**[AGREE]。**\n\n鉴于 R2-N1 指出 image token instruction-blindness 是架构必然结果，\"strict\" 一词过度暗示这是 learned specialization。建议改为 \"architecture-constrained functional division\" 或简单删除 \"strict\"。\n\n---\n\n### R2 Missing References\n\n**[AGREE]。** 以下引用均与论文直接相关，应当纳入：\n1. \"What Do Visual Tokens Really Encode?\" — 直接研究 VLM 中视觉 token 的信息内容\n2. \"Towards Interpreting Visual Information Processing in VLMs\" (ICLR 2025) — VLM 内部信息处理的解释性分析\n3. \"Seeing is Understanding\" — 特别是关于 causal mask 对视觉 token 信息流的影响\n4. \"Phase Diagram of Vision LLMs Inference\" — VLM 推理中的信息流分析\n5. \"Understanding Language Prior of LVLMs\" — 语言先验对 VLM 的影响\n6. AVA-VLA — VLA 注意力分析\n\n其中第 2、3、4 条与本文方法论最相关，应在 Related Work 中讨论。\n\n---\n\n## 回应 Reviewer 3\n\n---\n\n### R3-N1 [critical] 正文超出 8 页限制\n\n**[AGREE]，但严重程度需精确测量。**\n\n从论文结构看，main text 包含 6 个 section（Introduction 到 Discussion/Conclusion），其中 experiments.tex 非常密集（~178 行 LaTeX，含 2 个 table + 2 个 figure + 大量 paragraph）。Discussion + Conclusion 确实延伸到第 9 页。这是 CoRL desk rejection 的潜在理由。\n\nR3 估计超限 \"约 0.7 页\" 需精确验证——我观察到论文头部有压缩 spacing 的设置（main.tex line 16-20: 多个 \\setlength 命令减小 float/text 间距），这暗示作者已在努力压缩但仍未达标。\n\n不过我要补充：Conclusion 仅 3 行（conclusion.tex），且是 Discussion 内的 \\paragraph，所以超限部分主要是 Discussion 正文的 4 个 dense paragraphs。压缩空间确实存在。\n\n---\n\n### R3-N2 [critical] Abstract 和 Introduction 信息过载\n\n**[AGREE] on the problem, [REBUT] on severity — 我认为这是 should-fix 而非 must-fix。**\n\nAbstract 确实在约 200 words 内塞入了 13+ 个定量值（d = 0.48→0.64, d = 0.63--0.97, 2.5×, p = 0.033, R² +0.069, +46pp 等）。Introduction 第 17-21 行几乎逐字重复这些数字。这对读者不友好。\n\n但标注为 \"critical\" 我不同意——Abstract 过密是写作风格问题，不影响技术正确性或可复现性。顶会论文中定量密集的 abstract 并非罕见（尤其是 empirical analysis 类论文）。建议将具体数字移到 Introduction，Abstract 只保留 qualitative findings。这是 should-fix。\n\n---\n\n### R3-W1 [warning] Figure 1 不在第 1 页\n\n**[AGREE]。**\n\n论文的第一个 figure（fig_attention_profile，即 Figure 1）出现在 experiments section 中（experiments.tex line 112-125），对应 PDF 约第 6 页。对于一篇方法论复杂的分析论文，缺少第 1 页的 overview/teaser figure 是明显的呈现缺陷。一张展示 \"image tokens → readout token → action\" 信息流的 schematic diagram 可以极大帮助读者建立 mental model。\n\n---\n\n### R3-W2 [warning] 层编号标注错误\n\n**[AGREE]。这是一个明确的事实错误。**\n\nmethod.tex line 26:\n> \"L0 denotes the embedding layer output, L1--L31 the 32 transformer layers, and L32 the output after the final layer norm.\"\n\nL1 至 L31 仅包含 31 个索引（1,2,...,31），但论文声称这是 \"the 32 transformer layers\"。LLaMA-2-7B 确实有 32 个 transformer 层。如果 L0 = embedding output 且 L32 = final layer norm output，那么 32 个 transformer 层应为 L1--L32，文中写法有误。\n\n或者，如果采用 0-indexed 惯例（L0--L31 = 32 层），则 L0 不应被定义为 \"embedding layer output\"。当前定义存在不一致。\n\n---\n\n### R3-W3 [warning] 多篇引用使用 arXiv 而非已发表版本\n\n**[AGREE]。**\n\n引用验证显示以下 bib entries 使用 arXiv preprint 但已有正式发表版本：\n- kim2024_openvla (OpenVLA) — 已发表于 CoRL 2024\n- _source line 491 · 2026-05-20T16:07:56.815179+00:00_

1	Vision-language-action models (VLAs) fine-tune large language models on robot demonstrations, but what fine-tuning changes at the representational level remains unclear.
2	We decompose OpenVLA-7B across all 32 LLM layers using cross-instruction probes, attention analysis, and functional probes, with factorial trained/pretrained/untrained controls on LIBERO.
3	Three findings emerge.
4	First, VLA fine-tuning redistributes instruction attention from mid-depth to entry-exit layers, supported by seven complementary analyses including dose-response and counterfactual ablation; the pattern replicates across two causal-decoder VLA architectures.
5	Second, vision encoder representations show a middle-layer peak in action decodability, with training effects concentrated at the readout token ($R^2$ $+0.069$) rather than image-token geometry; two architectures with opposite untrained profiles both reach similar trained peaks ($\Delta = 0.003$).
6	Third, token-level analysis confirms expected functional specialization---image tokens remain instruction-blind while the readout token integrates multimodal information---consistent with the causal attention mask and serving as a framework sanity check.
7

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	What does fine-tuning change inside a Vision-Language-Action model (VLA)?
5	VLAs fine-tune large language models on robot demonstrations to produce actions from visual observations and language instructions~\cite{brohan2023_rt2,kim2024_openvla}, but the decoder-only causal mask prevents image tokens from attending to instructions~\cite{wang2025seeing,pei2025rethinking}.
6	Instruction information must reach action-generating positions through learned attention patterns, yet how fine-tuning reshapes these patterns remains uncharacterized.
7	
8	Recent behavioral studies reveal that VLAs rely disproportionately on visual input~\cite{grant2026}, with language instructions largely ignored by the action pathway~\cite{haon2025}.
9	These findings suggest internal specialization but do not explain \emph{how} instruction information reaches the action-generating readout token, \emph{what} attention strategy fine-tuning learns compared to language pretraining alone, or \emph{whether} training creates new representational structure or reconfigures inherited geometry.
10	Existing analyses have not combined systematic per-layer probing with factorial trained/pretrained/untrained controls, leaving the representational mechanisms of VLA fine-tuning opaque.
11	
12	We address this gap by decomposing OpenVLA-7B~\cite{kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024}, a VLA chaining a frozen DinoSigLIP vision backbone~\cite{zhai2023_siglip,oquab2024_dinov2}, an MLP projector, and Llama-2 7B~\cite{touvron2023_llama2}.
13	We extract representations from each of 32 LLM layers at both image-token and readout-token positions, and from external baselines (DINOv2~\cite{oquab2024_dinov2}, CLIP~\cite{radford2021_clip}).
14	We compare three LLM conditions (VLA-trained, pretrained Llama-2 base, and randomly initialized) crossed with instruction presence, and apply four complementary analyses: cross-instruction probes for token-level specialization, L1 attenti
…[truncated 1712 chars]

_source line 503 · 2026-05-20T16:08:14.800136+00:00_

1	\vspace{-2pt}
2	\section{Discussion}
3	\label{sec:discussion}
4	
5	\paragraph{Synthesis.}
6	VLA fine-tuning redistributes instruction attention rather than reshaping visual geometry: training effects concentrate at the readout ($R^2$ $+0.069$, attention $+46$pp) rather than images ($\Delta \leq 1.7\%$), explaining Grant et al.'s~\cite{grant2026} visual dominance finding ($\rho = 0.692$ for DINOv2, yet readout $R^2 = 0.678$ vs.\ $0.634$).
7	Seven analyses support L1 redistribution: \emph{interventional} (dose-response $d = 0.48 \to 0.64$; counterfactual $d = 0.63$--$0.97$), \emph{supporting} (gradient $0 \to 9 \to 24$ heads; ablation $+0.059$; trajectory $d = 0.65$), and \emph{constraining} ($p = 0.0018$; $z = -3.11$, $0$\textsuperscript{th} percentile; Appendix~\ref{sec:mechanistic_interpretation}--\ref{sec:functional_stages}).
8	
9	\paragraph{Limitations and scope.}
10	We study OpenVLA-7B on held-out LIBERO; attention patterns may differ across scales (Appendix~\ref{sec:ood_diagnostics}).
11	Both architectures are causal-decoder; flow-matching~\cite{black2024_pi0} and diffusion~\cite{ghosh2024_octo} VLAs use cross-attention, so findings may not generalize to non-autoregressive decoders.
12	Llama-2's 9/32 pretrained instruction heads may reflect text bias ($+15$-head delta isolates fine-tuning).
13	All analyses are open-loop, as in concurrent VLA representation studies~\cite{grant2026,haon2025}; however, representational analysis has independent design value---our entry-exit profile yields a concrete diagnostic (L1 attention entropy monitoring during fine-tuning), and the readout-vs-image dissociation directly informs token pruning strategies.
14	Closed-loop validation on downstream task success is an orthogonal contribution and a natural next step.
15

1	\paragraph{Conclusion.}
2	VLA fine-tuning redistributes instruction attention to entry-exit layers with L1 as the primary hub, consistent across two causal-decoder VLAs and supported by seven complementary analyses.
3	Training effects concentrate at the readout rather than image geometry, with a consistent reorganization pattern across both architectures studied. Code and data will be released upon acceptance.
4

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	We evaluate OpenVLA-7B~\cite{kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024} on LIBERO-Goal~\cite{liu2023_libero} (10 tasks, 50 demonstrations each).
8	Representations are extracted from six sources spanning the VLA pipeline (\S\ref{sec:repr_extraction}): DinoSigLIP+Projector, LLM image\_mean and readout tokens at each of 32 layers, DINOv2~\cite{oquab2024_dinov2} (\texttt{cls\_token}/\texttt{patch\_mean}), CLIP~\cite{radford2021_clip} (\texttt{patch\_mean}), and branch-split (DINOv2 1{,}024-D / SigLIP 1{,}152-D before fusion).
9	Three LLM conditions are compared: VLA-trained, pretrained Llama-2 base (no VLA fine-tuning), and untrained (6.74B parameters reinitialized to random weights), crossed with instruction presence (\S\ref{sec:model_data}).
10	RSA computes Spearman $\rho$ between cosine-distance representation RDMs and Euclidean-distance action RDMs, with 10{,}000 bootstrap CIs (\S\ref{sec:rsa}).
11	MLP probes (input $\to$ 256 $\to$ 7; Adam, lr${}=10^{-3}$, 50 epochs, early stopping) report mean $R^2$ (\S\ref{sec:probes}).
12	This yields $N = 63{,}228$ observations (after excluding terminal frames without next-action labels; $63{,}728$ total); RSA uses task-level blocking ($N_\text{eff} = 10$) and probes use demo-level train/test splits.
13	All patterns reproduce with a linear probe (Appendix~\ref{sec:linear_probe}).
14	
15	%------------------------------------------------------------------
16	\subsection{Framework Validation: Token-Level Specialization}
17	\label{sec:token_specialization}
18	
19	As a sanity check on the experimental framework, we train cross-instruction probes (\S\ref{sec:cross_instruction_probes}): given the same visual frame with each of 10 task instructions, we classify which instruction was present from per-layer activations.
20

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	We investigate how action-relevant structure is distributed across a VLA by combining four complementary analyses: cross-instruction probes, attention pattern analysis, RSA, and functional probes.
5	
6	%------------------------------------------------------------------
7	\subsection{Model and Data}
8	\label{sec:model_data}
9	
10	We study OpenVLA-7B~\cite{kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024}, chaining a frozen DinoSigLIP vision backbone~\cite{zhai2023_siglip,oquab2024_dinov2} (DINOv2 ViT-L/14 + SigLIP-SO400M), an MLP projector (2{,}176-D $\to$ 4{,}096-D), and Llama-2 7B~\cite{touvron2023_llama2} (6.74B parameters) that autoregressively decodes 7-D actions.
11	We use the base checkpoint pretrained on Open X-Embodiment~\cite{padalkar2023_oxe} (970K episodes spanning multiple robot platforms and task types) and evaluate on LIBERO-Goal~\cite{liu2023_libero} (10 tasks, 50 demonstrations each), which was not part of the pretraining mixture.
12	We compare three LLM conditions: (1)~VLA-trained (released checkpoint), (2)~Llama-2 base (pretrained backbone, no VLA fine-tuning), and (3)~untrained (6.74B parameters reinitialized via \texttt{from\_config()}); a fourth condition (task-specific fine-tuning on LIBERO) is compared in \S\ref{sec:learned_routing}.
13	All three share the same frozen vision encoder and projector.
14	
15	%------------------------------------------------------------------
16	\subsection{Representation Extraction}
17	\label{sec:repr_extraction}
18	
19	We extract representations from six sources spanning the VLA pipeline:
20	(a)~\textbf{DinoSigLIP+Projector}, the projector output (4{,}096-D), immediately before LLM processing;
21	(b)~\textbf{LLM image\_mean}, the mean image-token activation at each of 32 layers;
22	(c)~\textbf{LLM readout}, the hidden state at the final pre-action token position at each layer (located after both image and instruction tokens in the causal sequence, it can attend to all preceding context

_source line 513 · 2026-05-20T16:08:47.724371+00:00_

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related_work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Vision-language-action models.}
4	VLAs fine-tune large language models on robot demonstrations to produce action tokens conditioned on visual observations and language instructions~\cite{brohan2023_rt2,kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024,black2024_pi0}; recent work conditions action generation on dynamic visual reweighting~\cite{xiao2025ava}.
5	This architecture family inherits the transformer backbone and its multi-modal fusion mechanisms from vision-language models, but repurposes them for continuous control.
6	A central assumption is that fine-tuning injects action-relevant knowledge into pretrained representations; unlike NLP instruction tuning, which targets behavioral compliance~\cite{wei2022finetuned,ouyang2022training}, we test this at the representational level through per-layer, per-token-type decomposition.
7	
8	\paragraph{Mechanistic interpretability in transformers.}
9	Mechanistic analyses have identified residual stream communication~\cite{elhage2021}, induction heads for in-context learning~\cite{olsson2022}, complete circuits for specific tasks~\cite{wang2023_ioi,conmy2023}, causal tracing for factual recall~\cite{meng2022_rome}, and the tuned lens for per-layer prediction~\cite{belrose2023_tunedlens}, while feature-level methods have scaled to dictionary learning over millions of features~\cite{bills2023language,templeton2024scaling}.
10	Attention probing reveals that individual heads encode syntactic relations~\cite{clark2019}, capture depth-dependent patterns~\cite{vig2019}, and that a small subset accounts for most performance~\cite{voita2019}; Michel et al.~\cite{michel2019sixteen} showed most heads can be pruned without loss.
11	Our L1 analysis extends this to VLAs: we use functional ablation to study attention redistribution and find that 24 of 32 heads become instruction-dominant after fine-tuning, with progressive ablation yielding monotonic rather than threshold-like effects.
12	
1

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_15/projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/appendix.tex:902:The $2 {\times} 2$ trained-vs-untrained comparison reveals convergent reorganization: untrained OpenVLA collapses from L0 ($R^2 = 0.629$) to near-zero at mid-layers (L20: $0.086$, std $= 0.277$), while untrained Phi-3-V peaks at L6 ($0.578$) with gradual decline (std $= 0.068$)---qualitatively opposite untrained landscapes.

\"convergent reorganization across **the two architectures studied**\"\n- Conclusion: \"consistent across **two causal-decoder** VLAs\"\n\nqualification 非常一致。未发现任何 unqualified generalization。\n\n**(b) 更聚焦于已有证据的总结。\n\n---\n\n### R9-N2 [wont_fix] 无 closed-loop 机器人评估\n\n**[PARTIALLY_RESOLVED]** — 诚实承认但 framing 有问题\n\ndiscussion.tex lines 13-14:\n> \"All analyses are open-loop, as in concurrent VLA fine-tuning), and the readout-vs-image dissociation directly informs token pruning strategies. Closed-loop validation on downstream task success is **an orthogonal contribution** and a natural next step.\"\n\n**正面**：(1) 诚实承认 open-loop；(2) 引用同期工作（Grant, Häon）建立 precedent；(3) 给出两个具体的 actionable implications（L1 attention entropy 监控、token pruning）。\n\n**问题**：\"orthogonal contribution\" 措辞暗示 closed-loop validation 是独立的、可选的研究方向，而非验证本文 claims 的必要步骤。实际上，只有 closed-loop 实验才能验证 \"attention redistribution 对任务成功率的影响\" 这一关键问题。\"Orthogonal\" 一词过于 dismissive。建议改为 \"a complementary and necessary next step\" 或 \"an essential validation step that we leave for future work\"。\n\n此外，两个 actionable implications 仍未被实验验证——论文没有实际实现 L1 attention entropy 监控或 token pruning。这些仍是推测性建议。\n\n---\n\n### R9-W1 [critical→open] CoRL scope fit\n\n**[PARTIALLY_RESOLVED]**\n\n相比旧版的改善：\n1. real-robot、无 sim-to-real evidence\n\n**我的判断**：论文在 scope framing 上有明显改善，但核心缺陷（纯 open-loop 分析）无法仅通过 writing 修改解决。对 CoRL 而言，这仍是一个 borderline concern，但鉴于同期 VLA 解释性工作（Grant 2026, Häon 2025, Swann 2026）也是 自身。但这是一个修辞性 title convention（类似 \"Attention Is All You Need\" 中 attention 也不是 literal agent），且正文已全面使用 \"VLA fine-tuning redistributes\" 的准确措辞。\n\n**严重程度**：info。不影响接收决定。\n\n### NEW-2 [minor] \"Orthogonal contribution\" framing 过于 dismissive\n\n如上 R9-N2 分析所述。\"Orthogonal\" 暗示 closed-loop 验证与本文 claims 无关，而实际上它是验证本文 claims 实用价值的必要步骤。\n\n**严重程度**：warning。建议在 camera-ready 中修改措辞。\n\n### NEW-3 starting points suggests that mid-layer action specialization is a **robust attractor** of VLA training\" — 较强的因果暗示\n\n\"Robust attractor\" 暗示动力系统的稳定固定点，从 N=2 架构观察中得出这一结论偏强。但这在附录中，不影响主文 claims。\n\n**严重程度**：info。\n\n### NEW-4 [info] 页面压缩激进\n\nmain.tex 的 spacing 参数（\\textfloatsep=6pt, \\abovecaptionskip=2pt, \\vspace{-2pt} before §5）较为激进。Discussion 被压缩到 page 8 的下半部分。视觉上不影响可读性，但接近 CoRL 格式规范的边界。当前版本 main text 约 8 页，References 从 page 9 开始，符合页限。\n\n**严重程度**：info。在限制内。\n\n### 未引入重大新问题\n\nAbstract 精简后仍保留了三个 findings 的核心信息（redistributes attention、middle-layer peak、expected specialization as sanity check），加上关键数字（R² +0.069, Δ=0.003）。未丢失关键信息。跨架构 claim 弱化后，论文的核心贡献（attention redistribution phenomenon + systematic analysis framework）仍然成立。\n\n---\n\n## Strengths of Revision\n\n1. **语言一致性极高**：全文 grep 确认零残留 \"routing\" 和 \"strict\"。从 Title 到 Conclusion，\"redistributes instruction attention\" 贯穿始终。这是非常认真的修改。\n\n2. **Finding 3 的降格处理精准**：Abstract \"confirms expected\" + \"causal attention mask\" + \"framework sanity check\" 三重标注；Contribution (iii) 定位为 \"methodology validation\"；Section 4.2 title 保持 \"Framework Validation\"。没有过度削弱（sanity check 仍然有方法论价值），也没有残留 overclaiming。\n\n3. **跨架构限定一致彻底**：所有出现 cross-architecture claim 的位置（7+处）均加上 \"causal-decoder\" 或 \"the two architectures studied\" 限定。Limitations 明确列出 flow-matching/diffusion 架构。\n\n4. **Teaser figure α=0.05, 33-layer family）。\n\n7. **Related Work 显式区分**：Related Work lines 18-20 明确区分 Grant (\"what\" features dominate)、Häon (\"what\" semantic directions)、本文 (\"where\" across layers and token Finding 3 的 framing 修正使论文 claims 与 evidence 匹配（R4-N1 fully resolved）\n3. 跨架构 claim 的诚实限定增强了可信度（R9-N1 resolved）\n4. 多项技术修复（层编号、Cohen's d 双级、FDR 细节）提升了严谨性\n5. 新增 teaser figure 和 missing references 改善了整体呈现\n\n**未上调至 4.0 的原因**：\n1. 纯 open-loop 分析对 CoRL 仍是核心局限——论文仍无法回答 \"这些 attention patterns 对机器人实际执行有什么影响？\"\n2. vision tokens 的行为（overclaiming），或 (2) 描述模型的功能组织（vision modality 负责 encoding）。在 Abstract 和正文已明确标注为 sanity check 的前提下，Title 作为修辞性 shorthand 可以接受。如果 R2 仍有异议，可进一步讨论，但我认为当前处理已达到 reasonable balance。\n\n**(c) Contributions 列表**（Introduction line 21）：\n- Contribution (iii): \"a systematic framework for token-level representational analysis of VLAs with factorial controls, **including token specialization as a methodology validation**\"\n\nFinding 3 被明确定位为方法论验证（methodology validation），不再作为独立发现呈现。这是恰当的处理。\n\n---\n\n### R9-N1 [wont_fix] 跨架构泛化不足\n\n**[RESOLVED]** — claim 已恰当弱化\n\n**(a) 全文一致性**：关键位置均使用限定语：\n- Abstract: \"across two **causal-decoder** VLA architectures\"\n- Introduction line 18: \"two **causal-decoder** VLAs\"\n- Introduction line 21 (Contributions): \"**two causal-decoder** VLAs\" + \"the two **causal-decoder architectures studied**\"\n- experiments.tex line 71: \"consistent across **causal-decoder** architectures\"\n- experiments.tex line 73: \"convergent reorganization across **the two architectures studied**\"\n- Conclusion: \"consistent across **two causal-decoder** VLAs\"\n\nqualification 非常一致。未发现任何 unqualified generalization。\n\n**(b) Limitations**（discussion.tex lines 10-11）：明确指出 \"Both architectures are causal-decoder; **flow-matching [π₀] and diffusion [Octo] VLAs use cross-attention**, so findings may not generalize to non-autoregressive decoders.\" 直接回应了 R2-N3 关于缺少 flow-matching/diffusion 架构的批评。\n\n**(c) Discussion 预测**：已移除。旧版 Discussion 包含 \"cross-attention VLAs should lack L1 routing heads\" 等推测性预测。新版 Discussion 更聚焦于已有证据的总结。\n\n---\n\n### R9-N2 [wont_fix] 无 closed-loop 机器人评估\n\n**[PARTIALLY_RESOLVED]** — 诚实承认但 framing 有问题\n\ndiscussion.tex lines 13-14:\n> \"All analyses are open-loop, as in concurrent VLA representation studies [Grant, Häon]; however, representational analysis has independent design value—our entry-exit profile yields a concrete diagnostic (L1 attention entropy monitoring during fine-tuning), and the readout-vs-image dissociation directly informs token pruning strategies. Closed-loop validation on downstream task success is **an orthogonal contribution** and a natural next step.\"\n\n**正面**：(1) 诚实承认 open-loop；(2) 引用同期工作（Grant, Häon）建立 precedent；(3) 给出两个具体的 actionable implications（L1 attention entropy 监控、token pruning）。\n\n**问题**：\"orthogonal contribution\" 措辞暗示 closed-loop validation 是独立的、可选的研究方向，而非验证本文 claims 的必要步骤。实际上，只有 closed-loop 实验才能验证 \"attention redistribution 对任务成功率的影响\" 这一关键问题。\"Orthogonal\" 一词过于 dismissive。建议改为 \"a complementary and necessary next step\" 或 \"an essential validation step that we leave for future work\"。\n\n此外，两个 actionable implications 仍未被实验验证——论文没有实际实现 L1 attention entropy 监控或 token pruning。这些仍是推测性建议。\n\n---\n\n### R9-W1 [critical→open] CoRL scope fit\n\n**[PARTIALLY_RESOLVED]**\n\n相比旧版的改善：\n1. Discussion 现在提出了两个具体的 robotics-relevant implications（L1 entropy 监控、token pruning strategy），而非之前的宽泛预测\n2. Limitations 诚实承认了 open-loop 和架构局限\n3. Related Work 更好地定位了论文在 VLA 研究中的位置\n\n仍然存在的问题：\n1. 两个 actionable implications 均未经验证——仍是推测性建议\n2. \"representational analysis has independent design value\" 的论证力度对 CoRL 审稿人而言可能不足\n3. 无 LIBERO rollout、无 real-robot、无 sim-to-real evidence\n\n**我的判断**：论文在 scope framing 上有明显改善，但核心缺陷（纯 open-loop 分析）无法仅通过 writing 修改解决。对 CoRL 而言，这仍是一个 borderline concern，但鉴于同期 VLA 解释性工作（Grant 2026, Häon 2025, Swann 2026）也是 open-loop 分析，论文至少与该新兴子领域的标准一致。\n\n---\n\n### R9-W4 [warning] Cohen's d 独立性\n\n**[RESOLVED]**\n\nexperiments.tex line 87:\n> \"token-level Cohen's d = 0.65; **trajectory-level d = 2.943**, Appendix\"\n\n现在同时报告 token-level 和 trajectory-level d。trajectory-level d (2.943) 是在 demo 层面聚合后计算的，避免了 token-level 的独立性问题。两者同时报告使读者可以评估聚合层级对 effect size 的影响。\n\n---\n\n### R12-W6 [warning] FDR 校正细节\n\n**[RESOLVED]**\n\nexperiments.tex line 147:\n> \"surviving **Benjamini–Hochberg** FDR correction (**α = 0.05**, **33-layer family per metric**) at 29–30/33 layers.\"\n\n现在明确指定：(1) BH 方法、(2) α = 0.05、(3) 33-layer family per metric（即每个 metric 独立做 33 次比较的校正）。完整且透明。\n\n---\n\n### R12-DIV1 [warning] Title \"Language Routes\" 误导\n\n**[RESOLVED]**\n\n\"Language Routes\" → \"Language Reorganizes\"。\"Reorganizes\" 准确描述了 attention distribution 的重组现象，不暗示主动的信息转发机制。全文 grep 确认零残留 \"routing\"。\n\n---\n\n## 新问题检查\n\n### NEW-1 [minor] Title \"Language Reorganizes\" — \"Language\" 仍作为 agent\n\nTitle \"Vision Encodes, Language **Reorganizes**\" 中，\"Language\" 是语法主语，暗示语言 token 主动执行了 reorganization。更准确地说，是 **VLA fine-tuning** reorganizes attention patterns，而非 language tokens 自身。但这是一个修辞性 title convention（类似 \"Attention Is All You Need\" 中 attention 也不是 literal agent），且正文已全面使用 \"VLA fine-tuning redistributes\" 的准确措辞。\n\n**严重程度**：info。不影响接收决定。\n\n### NEW-2 [minor] \"Orthogonal contribution\" framing 过于 dismissive\n\n如上 R9-N2 分析所述。\"Orthogonal\" 暗示 closed-loop 验证与本文 claims 无关，而实际上它是验证本文 claims 实用价值的必要步骤。\n\n**严重程度**：warning。建议在 camera-ready 中修改措辞。\n\n### NEW-3 [info] 附录中 \"convergent\" 措辞较主文更强\n\n主文 (experiments.tex line 73): \"**consistent with** convergent reorganization\" — 恰当的 hedging\n附录 (appendix.tex line 904): \"This convergent outcome from divergent starting points suggests that mid-layer action specialization is a **robust attractor** of VLA training\" — 较强的因果暗示\n\n\"Robust attractor\" 暗示动力系统的稳定固定点，从 N=2 架构观察中得出这一结论偏强。但这在附录中，不影响主文 claims。\n\n**严重程度**：info。\n\n### NEW-4 [info] 页面压缩激进\n\nmain.tex 的 spacing 参数（\\textfloatsep=6pt, \\abovecaptionskip=2pt, \\vspace{-2pt} before §5）较为激进。Discussion 被压缩到 page 8 的下半部分。视觉上不影响可读性，但接近 CoRL 格式规范的边界。当前版本 main text 约 8 页，References 从 page 9 开始，符合页限。\n\n**严重程度**：info。在限制内。\n\n### 未引入重大新问题\n\nAbstract 精简后仍保留了三个 findings 的核心信息（redistributes attention、middle-layer peak、expected specialization as sanity check），加上关键数字（R² +0.069, Δ=0.003）。未丢失关键信息。跨架构 claim 弱化后，论文的核心贡献（attention redistribution phenomenon + systematic analysis framework）仍然成立。\n\n---\n\n## Strengths of Revision\n\n1. **语言一致性极高**：全文 grep 确认零残留 \"routing\" 和 \"strict\"。从 Title 到 Conclusion，\"redistributes instruction attention\" 贯穿始终。这是非常认真的修改。\n\n2. **Finding 3 的降格处理精准**：Abstract \"confirms expected\" + \"causal attention mask\" + \"framework sanity check\" 三重标注；Contribution (iii) 定位为 \"methodology validation\"；Section 4.2 title 保持 \"Framework Validation\"。没有过度削弱（sanity check 仍然有方法论价值），也没有残留 overclaiming。\n\n3. **跨架构限定一致彻底**：所有出现 cross-architecture claim 的位置（7+处）均加上 \"causal-decoder\" 或 \"the two architectures studied\" 限定。Limitations 明确列出 flow-matching/diffusion 架构。\n\n4. **Teaser figure 添加**：page 1 现在有 Figure 1（架构 overview + attention redistribution 图），显著改善了首页可读性。\n\n5. **Missing references 全部补充**：wang2025seeing, pei2025rethinking (causal mask papers), xiao2025ava (AVA-VLA), fan2026visual, neo2025interpreting, long2026understanding, wei2024phase (VLM token analysis) — Related Work 覆盖面显著增强。\n\n6. **技术修复完成**：层编号修正（L1-L32）、Cohen's d 双级报告（token + trajectory）、FDR 细节完整（BH, α=0.05, 33-layer family）。\n\n7. **Related Work 显式区分**：Related Work lines 18-20 明确区分 Grant (\"what\" features dominate)、Häon (\"what\" semantic directions)、本文 (\"where\" across layers and token positions)。\n\n---\n\n## Remaining Concerns\n\n| Issue | Status | 严重程度 | 备注 |\n|-------|--------|---------|------|\n| R9-N2 closed-loop | PARTIALLY_RESOLVED | warning | \"orthogonal contribution\" 措辞 dismissive，且 actionable implications 仍未验证 |\n| R9-W1 CoRL scope | PARTIALLY_RESOLVED | warning | 改善明显但核心缺陷（open-loop only）无法仅通过 writing 解决 |\n| NEW-1 \"Language\" as agent in title | info | — | 修辞性 convention，正文已准确 |\n| NEW-2 \"orthogonal\" framing | warning | — | 建议 camera-ready 修改 |\n| NEW-3 附录 \"robust attractor\" | info | — | 仅在附录，不影响主文 |\n\n---\n\n## Updated Overall Assessment\n\n**Overall**: 3.5 / 5（Borderline Accept，上调 0.5）\n**Confidence**: 4 / 5\n\n**评分变化理由**：\n\n从 3.0 上调至 3.5，基于以下改善：\n1. 因果语言的全面修正消除了最严重的 methodological criticism（R12-N1 fully resolved）\n2. Finding 3 的 framing 修正使论文 claims 与 evidence 匹配（R4-N1 fully resolved）\n3. 跨架构 claim 的诚实限定增强了可信度（R9-N1 resolved）\n4. 多项技术修复（层编号、Cohen's d 双级、FDR 细节）提升了严谨性\n5. 新增 teaser figure 和 missing references 改善了整体呈现\n\n**未上调至 4.0 的原因**：\n1. 纯 open-loop 分析对 CoRL 仍是核心局限——论文仍无法回答 \"这些 attention patterns 对机器人实际执行有什么影响？\"\n2. [Round 1 审稿请求]:
你是 Reviewer 1，负责 **方法严谨性 + 实验有效性 + 统计方法论** 审查。

## 论文信息
- **标题**: Vision Encodes, Language Reorganizes: Token-Level Representational Analysis of VLA Fine-Tuning
- **目标 Venue**: CoRL 2026
- **Deadline**: 2026-05-29
- **格式**: CoRL 2026 模板，double-blind，正文 8 页 + references + appendix（不计入页数）
- **匿名规则**: Double-blind
- **论文路径**: projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/main.tex
- **PDF 路径**: /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_15/projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/main.pdf

## 你的审查侧重

请重点审查以下方面：

1. **方法论严谨性**
   - RSA 方法的适用性和实施正确性（within-task pairing, distance metrics, bootstrap）
   - Probe 架构设计（MLP probe capacity, overfitting risk, train/test split）
   - 实验控制设计（trained/untrained/pretrained 三条件, instruction ablation）
   - Cross-architecture comparison 的有效性（OpenVLA vs TraceVLA 是否构成充分对比）

2. **统计方法论**
   - Bootstrap CI 和置换检验的正确应用
   - 多重比较校正（FDR）
   - 效应量报告（Cohen's d 的独立性假设是否满足）
   - N=10 tasks 下 RSA 的统计功效
   - 显著性标注的准确性

3. **实验有效性**
   - 消融实验设计（L1 head ablation）的因果推断力
   - Counterfactual instruction analysis 的可靠性
   - Dose-response 分析的说服力
   - Linear probe 复现作为 robustness check 的充分性

4. **内部一致性**
   - 正文与 Appendix 数据一致性
   - 不同分析方法的结论是否收敛
   - RSA vs CKA 反转的处理是否充分

请按照标准审稿格式输出：Summary → Strengths → Weaknesses (Must-Fix / Should-Fix / Minor) → Questions → Missing References → Overall Assessment (1-10 分 + 置信度)。

A Mathematical Analysis of the ICLR 2026 Reciprocal Reviewer Nomination Policy","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.17950"},{"title":"CoRL 2024 - Instruction for Reviews","url":"https://2024.corl.org/contributions/instruction-for-reviews"}]

I found information about CoRL 2026 reviewer guidelines and related submission information. Here's what I discovered:

## CoRL 2026 Reviewer Requirements

All submissions must include at least one author who agrees to serve as a CoRL reviewer, and they are qualified if they have at least one accepted publication at a previous robotics (CoRL/ICRA/IROS/RSS) or learning conference (ICLR/NeurIPS/ICML) or equivalent journal. Additionally, every author with 3 or more submissions must agree to serve as a reviewer. Submissions that do not meet this reciprocal review requirement may be desk-rejected.

## Review Criteria and Guidelines

Based on the available CoRL information, submissions should focus on a core robotics problem and demonstrate the relevance of proposed models, algorithms, datasets, and benchmarks to robotics. All submissions should include a Limitations section, explicitly describing limiting assumptions, failure modes, and other limitations of the results and experiments.

Based on the search results, submissions to CoRL are evaluated based on the significance and novelty of the results, either theoretical or empirical, and results are judged on the degree to which they have been objectively established and/or their potential for scientific and technological impact, as well as their relevance to robotic learning.

# Review Form Information - CoRL 2024

Based on the provided webpage content, **no complete review form with scoring dimensions and scales is presented**. 

The page titled "Instruction for Reviews" contains only evaluation guidance rather than the actual review form itself. It specifies:

## Available Criteria:

**Review Evaluation Focus:** Papers are assessed on "significance and novelty of the results, either theoretical or empirical" and "the degree to which they have been objectively established and/or their potential for scientific and technological impact."

**Desk Rejection Standards:** Papers may be rejected before full review for:
- Formatting violations
- Anonymity breaches  
- Scope issues (lacking connection to robot learning, either through stated intent or empirical testing on physical robots)

## Missing Information:

The document does not include:
- Numerical rating scales (e.g., 1-5, 1-10)
- Detailed scoring rubrics for each dimension
- Specific structured questions reviewers must answer
- Downloadable review forms or templates

To obtain the complete review form with scoring scales and detailed criteria, you would need to access additional CoRL 2024 resources or contact the conference organizers directly.

_source line 591 · 2026-05-20T16:26:29.932229+00:00_

# CoRL 2023 Review Form Criteria

Based on the provided content, the reviewer guidelines outline **six main evaluation dimensions**, though the complete scoring scales are not fully detailed in this document:

## Evaluation Criteria

1. **Originality** - "Are the problems or methods new?" Assessment of novelty and how the work differs from prior contributions.

2. **Quality** - "Is the submission technically sound?" Evaluation of technical rigor, evidence supporting claims, and completeness.

3. **Clarity** - "Is the submission clearly written?" Assessment of organization and whether it adequately informs readers.

4. **Significance** - "Are the results important?" Evaluation of impact and whether the work advances the field meaningfully.

5. **Relevance** - "Does the paper address a problem in robot learning?" Assessment of alignment with conference scope and audience interest.

6. **Limitations** - Evaluation of honestly reported limitations; authors should be treated "kindly" for acknowledging constraints.

## Notable Guidance

The guidelines emphasize that reviewers should provide **constructive, concrete feedback** and avoid common mistakes like rejecting papers solely for lacking novel perspectives, not beating prior state-of-the-art, or minor errors—unless these directly undermine the core contribution.

**Limitation:** The specific numerical rating scales for each dimension are not included in the provided document excerpt.

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	We investigate how action-relevant structure is distributed across a VLA by combining four complementary analyses: cross-instruction probes, attention pattern analysis, RSA, and functional probes.
5	
6	%------------------------------------------------------------------
7	\subsection{Model and Data}
8	\label{sec:model_data}
9	
10	We study OpenVLA-7B~\cite{kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024}, chaining a frozen DinoSigLIP vision backbone~\cite{zhai2023_siglip,oquab2024_dinov2} (DINOv2 ViT-L/14 + SigLIP-SO400M), an MLP projector (2{,}176-D $\to$ 4{,}096-D), and Llama-2 7B~\cite{touvron2023_llama2} (6.74B parameters) that autoregressively decodes 7-D actions.
11	We use the base checkpoint pretrained on Open X-Embodiment~\cite{padalkar2023_oxe} (970K episodes spanning multiple robot platforms and task types) and evaluate on LIBERO-Goal~\cite{liu2023_libero} (10 tasks, 50 demonstrations each), which was not part of the pretraining mixture.
12	We compare three LLM conditions: (1)~VLA-trained (released checkpoint), (2)~Llama-2 base (pretrained backbone, no VLA fine-tuning), and (3)~untrained (6.74B parameters reinitialized via \texttt{from\_config()}); a fourth condition (task-specific fine-tuning on LIBERO) is compared in \S\ref{sec:learned_routing}.
13	All three share the same frozen vision encoder and projector.
14	
15	%------------------------------------------------------------------
16	\subsection{Representation Extraction}
17	\label{sec:repr_extraction}
18

20	(a)~\textbf{DinoSigLIP+Projector}, the projector output (4{,}096-D), immediately before LLM processing;
21	(b)~\textbf{LLM image\_mean}, the mean image-token activation at each of 32 layers;
22	(c)~\textbf{LLM readout}, the hidden state at the final pre-action token position at each layer (located after both image and instruction tokens in the causal sequence, it can attend to all preceding context

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	We evaluate OpenVLA-7B~\cite{kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024} on LIBERO-Goal~\cite{liu2023_libero} (10 tasks, 50 demonstrations each).
8	Representations are extracted from six sources spanning the VLA pipeline (\S\ref{sec:repr_extraction}): DinoSigLIP+Projector, LLM image\_mean and readout tokens at each of 32 layers, DINOv2~\cite{oquab2024_dinov2} (\texttt{cls\_token}/\texttt{patch\_mean}), CLIP~\cite{radford2021_clip} (\texttt{patch\_mean}), and branch-split (DINOv2 1{,}024-D / SigLIP 1{,}152-D before fusion).
9	Three LLM conditions are compared: VLA-trained, pretrained Llama-2 base (no VLA fine-tuning), and untrained (6.74B parameters reinitialized to random weights), crossed with instruction presence (\S\ref{sec:model_data}).
10	RSA computes Spearman $\rho$ between cosine-distance representation RDMs and Euclidean-distance action RDMs, with 10{,}000 bootstrap CIs (\S\ref{sec:rsa}).
11	MLP probes (input $\to$ 256 $\to$ 7; Adam, lr${}=10^{-3}$, 50 epochs, early stopping) report mean $R^2$ (\S\ref{sec:probes}).
12	This yields $N = 63{,}228$ observations (after excluding terminal frames without next-action labels; $63{,}728$ total); RSA uses task-level blocking ($N_\text{eff} = 10$) and probes use demo-level train/test splits.
13	All patterns reproduce with a linear probe (Appendix~\ref{sec:linear_probe}).

1	\vspace{-2pt}
2	\section{Discussion}
3	\label{sec:discussion}
4	
5	\paragraph{Synthesis.}
6	VLA fine-tuning redistributes instruction attention rather than reshaping visual geometry: training effects concentrate at the readout ($R^2$ $+0.069$, attention $+46$pp) rather than images ($\Delta \leq 1.7\%$), explaining Grant et al.'s~\cite{grant2026} visual dominance finding ($\rho = 0.692$ for DINOv2, yet readout $R^2 = 0.678$ vs.\ $0.634$).
7	Cross-architecture comparison strengthens this picture: two VLAs with different backbones and opposite untrained profiles both arrive at similar mid-layer peaks ($\Delta = 0.003$), suggesting the redistribution reflects task-structure constraints rather than architecture-specific artifacts.
8	Seven analyses support L1 redistribution: \emph{interventional} (dose-response $d = 0.48 \to 0.64$; counterfactual $d = 0.63$--$0.97$), \emph{supporting} (gradient $0 \to 9 \to 24$ heads; ablation $+0.059$; trajectory $d = 0.65$), and \emph{constraining} ($p = 0.0018$; $z = -3.11$, $0$\textsuperscript{th} percentile; Appendix~\ref{sec:mechanistic_interpretation}--\ref{sec:functional_stages}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Limitations and scope.}
11	We analyze two causal-decoder VLAs on held-out LIBERO; while their starting points are maximally different (base LM vs.\ instruction-tuned LM), extending to flow-matching~\cite{black2024_pi0} and diffusion~\cite{ghosh2024_octo} VLAs with cross-attention is needed to test generality beyond the causal-decoder family.
12	Llama-2's 9/32 pretrained instruction heads may reflect text bias ($+15$-head delta isolates fine-tuning).
13	All analyses are open-loop, as in concurrent VLA representation studies~\cite{grant2026,haon2025}; however, representational analysis has independent design value---convergent mid-layer peaks suggest early-exit inference at ${\sim}1/3$ depth as a testable hypothesis, and the entry-exit profile enables L1 entropy monitoring as a fine-tuning diagnostic.
14	Closed-loop validation would stre

1	\paragraph{Use of Generative AI.}
2	Generative AI tools (Claude, GPT-4) assisted with code implementation and manuscript editing; all scientific content was produced by the authors.
3	
4	\appendix
5	
6	\section{Supplementary Material}
7	\label{sec:appendix}
8	
9	Unless noted otherwise, all analyses use OpenVLA-7B (\texttt{openvla/openvla-7b} on HuggingFace) evaluated on LIBERO-Goal (10 tasks $\times$ 50 demonstrations, $N = 63{,}228$ observations after excluding terminal frames).
10	Model conditions, representation extraction, metric definitions, and probe architectures follow \S\ref{sec:setup}--\S\ref{sec:probes}.
11	Cross-benchmark experiments (\S\ref{sec:cross_benchmark_appendix}) use LIBERO-Spatial with the same protocol (10 tasks $\times$ 50 demonstrations).
12	All experiments ran on 2$\times$ NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition GPUs (96\,GB each); random seed is 42 unless stated otherwise.
13	
14	\subsection{CKA Analysis Method}
15	\label{sec:cka_appendix}
16	
17	We employ Centered Kernel Alignment (CKA)~\cite{kornblith2019} alongside RSA as a complementary perspective.
18	While RSA captures local geometric alignment via rank-order preservation, CKA measures global representational similarity through kernel-based comparisons.
19	
20	We compute both \emph{linear CKA}, which uses a linear kernel and is sensitive to second-order statistics, and \emph{RDM kernel CKA}, which operates on the same distance matrices as RSA but aggregates them differently.
21	Using both variants tests whether conclusions about representation structure are robust to the choice of similarity metric.
22	
23	Linear CKA between high-dimensional representations (4{,}096-D for LLM layers) and the 7-D action space is subject to a known dimension mismatch: the linear kernel in the low-dimensional space may not capture the same structure as in the high-dimensional space.
24	RDM kernel CKA avoids this issue by operating on distance matrices of equal size regardless of the original feat
…[truncated 13847 chars]

221	\subsection{Pretrained LLM Representation Quality Under Visual Input}
222	\label{sec:ood_diagnostics}
223	
224	A potential concern with the three-condition comparison (\S\ref{sec:model_data}) is that the pretrained Llama-2 backbone never encountered projected visual tokens during pretraining, so its representations of such input could be degenerate.
225	We evaluate three diagnostics to assess representation quality.
226	Activation magnitude grows from $16.5$ (L0) to $90.2$ (L31) for Llama-2 base, compared to $16.5 \to 51.7$ for the VLA-trained model; the pretrained LLM produces larger but non-exploding activations with no evidence of collapse.
227	Per-layer cosine similarity between L0 and deeper layers decreases progressively from $1.0$ to $\approx 0.28$ at L25, consistent with gradual differentiation rather than catastrophic out-of-distribution behavior.
228	Top-10 principal component variance explained remains stable across all layers ($0.59$--$0.74$), with no low-rank collapse.
229	These diagnostics indicate that the pretrained LLM produces structured, full-rank representations of visual input despite never encountering projected visual tokens during pretraining (Figure~\ref{fig:ood_diagnostics}). These results support the validity of the three-condition

600	\subsubsection{Multi-Layer Attention Patterns}

604	The full profile (Figure~\ref{fig:multilayer_attention}) reveals three qualitatively distinct patterns: the untrained model maintains 0 instruction-dominant heads at all 32 layers; Llama-2 base shows a mid-depth peak (L9--L16: 15--22 heads), consistent with typical autoregressive LM processing depth; VLA fine-tuning produces an entry-exit pattern (L0--L1: 21--24 heads, L31: 18 heads, intermediate layers 0--11).
